ArHa: A globally standardised, relational database of arenavirus and hantavirus sampling in wild small mammal populations

Authors: David G. Simons, Ricardo Rivero, Grant Rickard, Ana Guiote, Harry Gordon, Gregory Milne, David W. Redding, Stephanie Seifert
Original abstract (English)
Arenaviruses and hantaviruses are globally distributed zoonotic pathogens primarily hosted by small mammals. Efforts to map their ecological drivers and predict spillover risk are hindered by the fragmentation of historical surveillance data across the primary literature, compounded by shifting host nomenclature and variable geographic precision. We present ArHa, a database of 123,101 host occurrence and pathogen diagnostic records derived from a systematic review of published wild small mammal sampling. The dataset standardises host and pathogen taxonomies, resolves records of the same animals published in more than one paper, and classifies the spatial resolution and reliability of each coordinate to support bias-aware spatial epidemiology. To accurately preserve complex study designs, such as multiple diagnostic assays or genetic sequences derived from a single host, ArHa is structured as a four-tier relational database. This architecture handles many-to-one relationships and supports linkage without data duplication. The dataset is distributed as compressed Apache Parquet files for programmatic analysis and as a Darwin Core Archive, so that it can be read by standard biodiversity data tools. An accompanying interactive web application, the ArHa Database Explorer, allows users to filter, visualise, and export subsets of the database without requiring programming expertise. All data, extraction scripts, and the interactive web application are openly accessible via a permanent Zenodo repository.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Arenaviruses and hantaviruses are globally distributed zoonotic pathogens primarily hosted by small mammals.
Evidence base and method
Efforts to map their ecological drivers and predict spillover risk are hindered by the fragmentation of historical surveillance data across the primary literature, compounded by shifting host nomenclature and variable geographic precision.
Consensus and conclusions
We present ArHa, a database of 123,101 host occurrence and pathogen diagnostic records derived from a systematic review of published wild small mammal sampling.
Controversies and gaps
The dataset standardises host and pathogen taxonomies, resolves records of the same animals published in more than one paper, and classifies the spatial resolution and reliability of each coordinate to support bias-aware spatial epidemiology.
Research and practice implications
To accurately preserve complex study designs, such as multiple diagnostic assays or genetic sequences derived from a single host, ArHa is structured as a four-tier relational database.

汉坦病毒:一种新兴的全球人畜共患威胁——从分子基础到临床管理和公共卫生视角

Hantavirus: An Emerging Global Zoonotic Threat—From Molecular Basis to Clinical Management and Public Health Perspectives
作者： Mohd Abid, Ghulam Muheyuddeen Ghulam, Saqib Tanveer, Mohd Asif
摘要中文翻译汉坦病毒 感染是一种新出现的人畜共患病毒性疾病,由汉坦病毒科汉坦病毒引起,主要通过接触受感染的啮齿动物尿液、粪便、唾液或受污染的气溶胶传播给人类。该疾病主要有两种临床形式:肾综合征出血热 (HFRS),流行于亚洲和欧洲;汉坦病毒肺综合征 (HPS)/汉坦病毒 心肺综合征 (HCPS),主要报告于美洲。汉坦病毒的历史认可始于朝鲜战争期间(1950-1953 年),当时数千名士兵患上了朝鲜出血热。1978年,李浩旺从黑线姬鼠中分离出汉滩病毒,标志着一项重大科学突破。在接下来的几十年里,人们发现了多种汉坦病毒病毒,包括首尔病毒、普马拉病毒、多布拉瓦-贝尔格莱德病毒、辛诺布尔病毒和安第斯病毒,每种病毒都与不同的啮齿动物 储存宿主 和临床表现相关。1993 年,美国四角地区的 暴发 出现了一个重大的流行病学里程碑,导致了 Sin Nombre 病毒的发现。不断发展的城市化、气候变化、森林砍伐以及人类与啮齿动物之间更密切的互动导致了 21 世纪汉坦病毒感染在全球范围内的出现。分子诊断学的进步,包括逆转录聚合酶链反应 (RT-PCR) 和血清学检测,增强了疾病 监测 和早期 诊断 的能力。本综述重点介绍了历史上的 进化、流行病学、传播 以及汉坦病毒感染在全球范围内日益增长的公共卫生意义。关键词:汉坦病毒 感染;肾综合征出血热 (HFRS);汉坦病毒肺
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
汉坦病毒 感染是一种新出现的人畜共患病毒性疾病,由汉坦病毒科汉坦病毒引起,主要通过接触受感染的啮齿动物尿液、粪便、唾液或受污染的气溶胶传播给人类。

共识与结论
该疾病主要有两种临床形式:肾综合征出血热 (HFRS),流行于亚洲和欧洲;汉坦病毒肺综合征 (HPS)/汉坦病毒 心肺综合征 (HCPS),主要报告于美洲。

不断发展的城市化、气候变化、森林砍伐以及人类与啮齿动物之间更密切的互动导致了 21 世纪汉坦病毒感染在全球范围内的出现。
科研与实践启示
本综述重点介绍了历史上的 进化、流行病学、传播 以及汉坦病毒感染在全球范围内日益增长的公共卫生意义。

Hantavirus: An Emerging Global Zoonotic Threat—From Molecular Basis to Clinical Management and Public Health Perspectives

Authors: Mohd Abid, Ghulam Muheyuddeen Ghulam, Saqib Tanveer, Mohd Asif
Original abstract (English)
Hantavirus infection is an emerging zoonotic viral disease caused by hantaviruses of the family Hantaviridae, primarily transmitted to humans through exposure to infected rodent urine, feces, saliva, or contaminated aerosols. The disease presents mainly in two clinical forms: Hemorrhagic Fever with Renal Syndrome (HFRS), prevalent in Asia and Europe, and Hantavirus Pulmonary Syndrome (HPS)/Hantavirus Cardiopulmonary Syndrome (HCPS), predominantly reported in the Americas. The historical recognition of hantavirus began during the Korean War (1950–1953), when thousands of soldiers developed Korean hemorrhagic fever. In 1978, Ho-Wang Lee isolated the Hantaan virus from Apodemus agrarius, marking a major scientific breakthrough. Subsequent decades witnessed the identification of multiple hantavirus species, including Seoul, Puumala, Dobrava-Belgrade, Sin Nombre, and Andes viruses, each associated with distinct rodent reservoirs and clinical manifestations. A major epidemiological milestone occurred in 1993 with the outbreak of Hantavirus Pulmonary Syndrome in the Four Corners region of the United States, leading to the discovery of Sin Nombre virus. Increasing urbanization, climate change, deforestation, and closer human–rodent interactions have contributed to the global emergence of hantavirus infections in the 21st century. Advances in molecular diagnostics, including reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction (RT-PCR) and serological testing, have enhanced disease surveillance and early diagnosis. This review highlights the historical evolution, epidemiology, transmission, and growing public health significance of hantavirus infection worldwide. Keywords: Hantavirus infection; Hemorrhagic Fever with Renal Syndrome (HFRS); Hantavirus Pulmonary
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Hantavirus infection is an emerging zoonotic viral disease caused by hantaviruses of the family Hantaviridae, primarily transmitted to humans through exposure to infected rodent urine, feces, saliva, or contaminated aerosols.
Evidence base and method
Advances in molecular diagnostics, including reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction (RT-PCR) and serological testing, have enhanced disease surveillance and early diagnosis.
Consensus and conclusions
The disease presents mainly in two clinical forms: Hemorrhagic Fever with Renal Syndrome (HFRS), prevalent in Asia and Europe, and Hantavirus Pulmonary Syndrome (HPS)/Hantavirus Cardiopulmonary Syndrome (HCPS), predominantly reported in the Americas.
Controversies and gaps
Increasing urbanization, climate change, deforestation, and closer human–rodent interactions have contributed to the global emergence of hantavirus infections in the 21st century.
Research and practice implications
This review highlights the historical evolution, epidemiology, transmission, and growing public health significance of hantavirus infection worldwide.

新闻简报游轮上的汉坦病毒暴发 引起了重大关注和经济影响。暴发 于 2025 年 4 月上旬开始运营,于 2025 年 5 月 2 日首次悬挂旗帜,涉及从阿根廷飞往欧洲的大西洋航线上的 150 名乘客。世界卫生组织 (WHO) 报告了三人死亡、六人确诊感染以及其他疑似病例,症状包括发烧、胃肠道问题和严重呼吸道并发症等。美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 将 暴发 归类为低级 3 级紧急响应。WHO 表示,全球风险仍然较低,因为汉坦病毒的传播性比 COVID-19 或流感低。暴发导致邮轮股票下跌,包括嘉年华(CCL)、挪威邮轮(NCLH)和皇家加勒比(RCL),隔夜各跌幅高达1%。包括唐纳德·特朗普总统在内的美国政府正在密切关注事态发展,并计划发布完整报告。当局仍在调查传播途径,涉及的病毒是安第斯毒株,该毒株与有限的人际传播有关。暴发 引起了投资者的担忧,他们将其与 COVID-19 大流行对旅游业的影响进行了比较。
查看新闻五要素
时间
The outbreak was first flagged on May 2, 2025, with symptoms developing between April 6 and April 28, 2025. The event date is September 01, 2025, when the cruise ship "Disney Adventure" set sail. The publication date is October 28, 2026.
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在 MV Hondius 号游轮上,该游轮于 2025 年 4 月上旬从阿根廷乌斯怀亚出发,停泊在佛得角附近。受影响的人群包括从阿根廷飞往欧洲的大西洋航线上的 150 名乘客。
事件
MV Hondius 号上的汉坦病毒暴发 导致 3 人死亡、6 人确诊感染以及其他疑似病例。世界卫生组织 (WHO) 于 2025 年 5 月 2 日标记了该群急性呼吸道疾病病例,疾病预防控制中心 (CDC) 将 暴发 列为 3 级紧急响应。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 导致 3 人死亡、6 人确诊感染,还有其他疑似病例。WHO 指出,全球风险仍然较低,汉坦病毒 的传播性比 COVID-19 或流感低。CDC 将 暴发 归类为低级 3 级紧急响应。
应对、状态与不确定性
当局仍在调查病毒是如何在船上传播的。世界卫生组织和疾病预防控制中心已发出警报,美国政府正在研究 暴发。总体风险评估为低,暴发 被认为受到主管部门的控制。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

RCL, NCLH, CCL: Cruise Stocks Get Hit As Rare Hantavirus Outbreak Sparks Travel Fears - Stocktwits

News Brief
A hantavirus outbreak on a cruise ship has caused significant concern and economic impact. The outbreak, which began in early April 2025, was first flagged on May 2, 2025, involving 150 passengers on an Atlantic voyage from Argentina toward Europe. The World Health Organization (WHO) reported three deaths, six confirmed infections, and additional suspected cases, with symptoms ranging from fever and gastrointestinal issues to severe respiratory complications. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) classified the outbreak as a low-level Level 3 emergency response. The WHO stated that the global risk remains low, as hantaviruses are less transmissible than COVID-19 or influenza. The outbreak has led to a decline in cruise stocks, including Carnival (CCL), Norwegian Cruise Line (NCLH), and Royal Caribbean (RCL), with each dropping up to 1% overnight. The U.S. administration, including President Donald Trump, is closely monitoring the situation and plans to release a full report. Authorities are still investigating the transmission route, and the virus involved is the Andes strain, which is associated with limited human-to-human spread. The outbreak has raised fears among investors, who compare it to the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic on the travel industry.
Original source text (English)
10 14.5M 1.2K Advertisement|Remove ads. A hantavirus outbreak aboard a cruise ship has resulted in three deaths and raised global health concerns. Hundreds of people watch as the cruise ship "Disney Adventure" sets sail in the southwestern Baltic Sea, Germany, on September 01, 2025. (Photo by Jens Büttner/picture alliance via Getty Images) - WHO said affected passengers developed symptoms ranging from fever and stomach issues to severe respiratory complications. - Carnival, Norwegian Cruise Line, and Royal Caribbean declined by up to 1% overnight. - The CDC classified the hantavirus outbreak as a low-level Level 3 emergency response. U.S. cruise stocks, including Carnival (CCL), Norwegian Cruise Line (NCLH), and Royal Caribbean (RCL), all slipped overnight, causing investor concern after a sudden health scare linked to a South Atlantic voyage rattled confidence in an already sensitivity-prone travel sector. A luxury cruise voyage has turned into a major health incident after a hantavirus outbreak aboard the MV Hondius led to three deaths and growing international concern. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), a cluster of acute respiratory illness cases was first flagged on May 2, involving passengers on a cruise ship carrying 147 people. The vessel, which departed Ushuaia, Argentina, in early April, has been anchored off Cabo Verde as authorities coordinate response efforts. WHO noted that symptoms among affected passengers developed between April 6 and April 28, beginning with fever and gastrointestinal distress before rapidly escalating into pneumonia, respiratory failure, and shock in severe cases. WHO said hantaviruses usually spread through infected rodents or contaminated areas. In rare cases, the Andes strain can spread between people in close-contact settings such as shared cabins. Despite the severity of the onboard outbreak, the WHO continues to assess the overall global risk as low. Although global risk assessments remain low, the psychological impact on investors has been evident. Cruise operators' stocks, such as Carnival, Norwegian Cruise Line, and Royal Caribbean, declined by up to 1% overnight after a 2% to 3% decline in the regular session. For many investors, the situation parallels the COVID-19 pandemic, which upended the entire travel industry. Though the U.S. cruise sector has largely recovered from the operational collapse caused by the COVID-19 pandemic, the industry’s largest operators continue to grapple with the long-term consequences of massive borrowing accumulated during the shutdown years. While Royal Caribbean has emerged as the standout performer with returns exceeding 233% over the past five years, rivals CCL and NCLH continue to battle elevated debt burdens and margin pressure. NCLH, in particular, has plunged nearly 40% over the last five years. To make matters worse, last week the company warned that softer travel demand and geopolitical uncertainty are weighing on bookings in key markets and unveiled additional cost-cutting measures to stabilize profits. However, according to a Reuters report, the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention has categorized the hantavirus outbreak as a Level 3 emergency response, its lowest emergency alert level. So far this year, NCLH and CCL stocks have plunged nearly 23% and 12%, respectively, while RCL stock has gained 0.7%. For updates and corrections, email newsroom[at]stocktwits[dot]com. Comments posted here will also appear on symbol pages. Read about our editorial guidelines and ethics policy Next article Inovio is the most direct hantavirus play based on earlier DNA vaccine studies targeting Andes, Hantaan, and Puumala strains. Trump holds a press conference announcing U.S. Vice President Mike Pence to lead the effort combating the spread of the coronavirus on February 26, 2020. (Photo by Yasin Ozturk/Anadolu Agency via Getty Images) - Trump said that the administration will release a full report on a Hantavirus on Friday. - The outbreak aboard the Dutch-flagged cruise ship Hondius has resulted in three deaths, six confirmed infections and additional suspected cases. - Moderna drew attention due to its hantavirus vaccine partnership with Korea University. Shares of vaccine makers and biodefense companies rallied overnight on Friday after U.S. President Donald Trump addressed a deadly hantavirus outbreak aboard a cruise ship and promised a full update on the situation. Overnight, Emergent BioSolutions (EBS) climbed 3%, while Inovio Pharmaceuticals (INO) and Novavax (NVAX) each gained 2% and Moderna (MRNA) rose 1%. Trump said late Thursday that the administration was closely studying the outbreak from the Dutch-flagged cruise ship Hondius, where three passengers have died and several others became infected. “It’s very much, we hope, under control,” Trump said, adding that a full report will be released on Friday. The outbreak has triggered six confirmed infections and additional suspected cases aboard the vessel, which had 150 passengers onboard during an Atlantic voyage from Argentina toward Europe. Authorities are still investigating how the virus spread on board. Singapore has already isolated two passengers from the ship due to concerns about possible exposure during the trip. The World Health Organization (WHO) also issued an alert on the outbreak, although officials said that the global risk remains low and that hantavirus is less transmissible than COVID-19 or influenza. The virus involved is said to be the Andes strain, one of the few hantavirus variants associated with limited human-to-human transmission. Moderna is viewed as one of the U.S. government’s key vaccine platforms for outbreaks. Since 2023, the company has worked with Korea University’s Vaccine Innovation Center on an mRNA-based hantavirus vaccine candidate, with preclinical studies earlier this year showing protection against hantavirus infection in mice. In 2024, Moderna received a $176 million award backed by the Biomedical Advanced Research and Development Authority (BARDA) in 2024 for the development of mRNA-based pandemic influenza vaccines and outbreak preparedness programs. Moderna’s partnership with the Coalition for Epidemic Preparedness Innovations (CEPI) is also in focus, which previously said mRNA could help shorten vaccine-development timelines to as little as 100 days during outbreaks. Inovio, meanwhile, has the clearest hantavirus link among the stocks rallying overnight. The company previously participated in several hantavirus DNA vaccine studies targeting Hantaan, Puumala and Andes hantavirus strains. One National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases-backed study evaluated an Andes virus DNA vaccine to prevent hantavirus pulmonary syndrome, while another U.S. Army-supported study examined mixed hantavirus DNA vaccines. While none of Inovio’s hantavirus vaccine programs produced a commercial product, the outbreak revived speculative interest in companies with prior hantavirus research. Investors frequently rotate into Novavax during virus headlines due to the company’s vaccine-development platform and history of rapidly developing candidates for pathogens such as Ebola, MERS and Zika. The company’s protein-based nanoparticle platform and Matrix-M adjuvant tech can generate vaccine candidates against emerging infectious diseases, already used in marketed COVID-19 and malaria vaccines. Emergent BioSolutions, on the other hand, is often viewed as one of the U.S. government’s primary biodefense and medical-countermeasure contractors during public-health emergencies. The company has existing relationships with BARDA and makes products based on Anthrax, Ebola, smallpox, mpox and viral hemorrhagic fevers. Emergent previously received BARDA task orders for viral hemorrhagic fever therapeutics and has also participated in manufacturing efforts during outbreaks, including Ebola and Zika. On Stocktwits, retail sentiment for INO and NVAX was ‘extremely bullish’ amid ‘extremely high’ message volume, while MRNA saw ‘bullish’ sentiment with ‘high’ message volume. EBS also recorded ‘extremely bullish’ sentiment, though message volume remained ‘normal.’ Over the past year, MRNA has surged 102%, followed by EBS up 93% and NVAX higher by 55%, while INO has declined 22%. Jefferies, Stifel and Evercore ISI all raised their price targets on Datadog after what analysts broadly described as a solid “beat and raise” quarter. The firms cited stronger-than-anticipated demand, improving guidance and continued momentum in both core and AI-related products. 冷冻电镜揭示 Andes汉坦病毒如何抓住其人体细胞受体

Cryo-EM reveals how Andes hantavirus grips its human cell receptor
新闻简报阿尔伯特·爱因斯坦医学院的科学家与巴斯德研究所和路易斯安那州立大学什里夫波特健康中心合作,利用冷冻电子显微镜揭示了安第斯山脉汉坦病毒如何与其人类细胞受体 PCDH1 结合。这一发现于 2026 年 10 月 28 日发表,强调了该病毒的独特结构,其方形刺突蛋白需要整个结构才能附着在受体上。这些发现可能会导致针对安第斯病毒和其他汉坦病毒病毒的新疗法。今年早些时候,一艘游轮上的 暴发 导致 3 人死亡和 13 人患病,凸显了 治疗 缺乏经过批准的情况。该病毒通过密切接触传播并导致严重的呼吸道疾病,目前尚无 疫苗 可用。研究小组此前已确定刺突蛋白对于感染至关重要,但直到现在还无法确定确切的结合机制。这项研究可能有助于通过针对病毒在感染初始阶段的脆弱性来制定 抗病毒药物 策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于2026年10月28日,游轮暴发发生在今年早些时候。
地点与对象
暴发 游轮将乘客带到了一个未指定的地点,这项研究是由阿尔伯特·爱因斯坦医学院、巴斯德研究所和路易斯安那州立大学什里夫波特健康中心的研究人员进行的。
事件
科学家利用冷冻电子显微镜揭示了安第斯山脉汉坦病毒如何识别并结合人类细胞受体 PCDH1,这对于病毒的进入和复制至关重要。
规模、影响与风险
今年早些时候,一艘安第斯山脉汉坦病毒游轮上的 暴发 事故导致 3 人死亡、13 人患病。该病毒可引起严重的呼吸道疾病,并通过密切接触在人与人之间传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究提供了对该病毒作用机制和潜在治疗靶点的见解。但安第斯病毒尚无获批的疫苗或治疗,研究仍处于探索阶段。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Cryo-EM reveals how Andes hantavirus grips its human cell receptor

News Brief
Scientists at Albert Einstein College of Medicine, in collaboration with Institut Pasteur and LSU Health Shreveport, have uncovered how the Andes hantavirus binds to its human cell receptor, PCDH1, using cryo-electron microscopy. This discovery, published on October 28, 2026, highlights the virus's unique structure, with a square-shaped spike protein requiring the entire structure to attach to the receptor. The findings could lead to new therapies against Andes virus and other hantaviruses. Earlier this year, an outbreak on a cruise ship resulted in three deaths and 13 illnesses, underscoring the lack of approved treatments. The virus spreads through close contact and causes severe respiratory illness, with no current vaccines available. The research team previously identified the spike protein as essential for infection but could not determine the exact binding mechanism until now. This study may help in developing antiviral strategies by targeting the virus's vulnerability during the initial stages of infection.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Cryo-EM reveals how Andes hantavirus grips its human cell receptor by October 28, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 4 mins read 0 Cryo-EM reveals how Andes hantavirus grips its human cell receptor Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Scientists at Albert Einstein College of Medicine, working with collaborators at Institut Pasteur and LSU Health Shreveport, have published a study in Nature showing how the Andes hantavirus recognizes its target on a human cell. The work arrives against a sobering backdrop: earlier this year, an outbreak of Andes hantavirus on a cruise ship killed three passengers and sickened 13, underscoring the absence of approved vaccines or treatments for the virus. The new findings, the researchers say, could inform the development of effective therapies against Andes virus and other hantaviruses. Hantaviruses are a large family of viruses carried mainly by rodents, and they can cause serious and sometimes fatal diseases in people, including hantavirus pulmonary syndrome, a severe respiratory illness that can rapidly lead to breathing failure. Human infections occur mainly when people inhale airborne virus particles from the urine, droppings, or saliva of infected rodents. Of the more than 20 hantaviruses known to sicken humans, Andes virus is the only one known to spread from person to person, typically through close and prolonged contact. This year’s cruise ship outbreak provided a dramatic example of that capability. The research team had previously established that infection depends on the spike protein on the Andes virus surface latching onto a receptor called PCDH1 on the surface of human cells. That receptor essentially serves as a doorway for the virus to enter the cell and then multiply inside it. What remained unanswered was a precise mechanistic question: how does the spike protein actually latch onto PCDH1? Eight years ago the group discovered that Andes virus uses this particular surface protein to infect human cells, but the exact recognition mechanism eluded them. “Eight years ago, we discovered that Andes virus infects human cells by using a particular protein on the cell’s surface, called a receptor, but we couldn’t figure out exactly how the virus recognized it,” said co-corresponding author Kartik Chandran, Ph.D., professor of microbiology and immunology at Einstein. “Our latest work solves this puzzle: We reveal a new way in which a virus can capture its receptor during infection and also suggest how we can fight back with therapies targeted to exploit the resulting viral vulnerability.” The answer lies in an unusual architecture. The spike protein of SARS-CoV-2, the virus that causes COVID-19, is built from three identical protein units arranged in a triangle, each carrying a separate receptor-binding domain that latches onto its receptor, ACE2. The Andes virus spike, by contrast, is an arrangement of four identical protein units locked together into a square. Unlike the SARS-CoV-2 and ACE2 pairing, a single protein unit from the Andes virus cannot bind to its receptor on its own; instead, the entire square-shaped spike is required to capture PCDH1. To see what happens at the very beginning of infection, when the Andes virus first encounters a human cell, the researchers used cryo-electron microscopy to reveal the structure in sharp detail. That step was accomplished only after the team developed innovative new methods, including an artificial intelligence-designed scaffold, that enabled the square-shaped Andes virus spike to hold together in a test tube. “The best way to prove a rare bird exists is to take a picture of it,” said Pablo Guardado-Calvo, Ph.D., deputy director of the Virology Department and head of the Structural Biology of Infectious Diseases unit at Institut Pasteur. “When we mixed this engineered Andes virus spike with PCDH1 and looked at it under the microscope, we knew we had something special.” The structures showed that a single molecule of PCDH1 embeds itself into the heart of the spike by binding into a deep groove, or pocket, at its center. The researchers also resolved molecular specifics of the interaction that will be important for designing treatments intended to block it. “The depth of this pocket also helps explain how the virus limits antibody access,” said Rohit Jangra, Ph.D., assistant professor of microbiology and immunology at LSU Health Shreveport. “What’s striking is how the same pocket can accommodate the receptor while restricting antibody access. PCDH1’s long, slender shape lets it reach deep inside, but most antibodies are too bulky to follow.” The team then examined several antibodies isolated from human hantavirus patients that had previously been shown to block the Andes virus-PCDH1 binding interaction. They found that some of these antibodies could indeed bind close enough to the entrance of the receptor-binding pocket to prevent PCDH1 from entering it, but they could not fit as deeply into the pocket as PCDH1 does. According to Chandran, this suggests a clever way in which Andes virus can escape receptor-blocking antibodies: the virus mutates so that antibodies cannot destroy it, yet those changes do not affect its ability to bind to the receptor, a kind of molecular “friend-or-foe” identification system. That same antibody-escape strategy, however, creates a vulnerability the team intends to exploit. Although human antibodies are limited to a certain architecture, scientists can create “long and skinny” molecules, suggesting an approach that could block the PCDH1 pocket and offer a blueprint for the development of new antiviral medicines that the researchers describe as urgently needed for Andes virus and its close relative in the United States and Canada, Sin Nombre virus. “This work creates a unified picture of the hantavirus-receptor interaction that could help us conceive and discover new protective strategies that work broadly against these high-consequence pathogens,” said Dr. Guardado-Calvo. The paper, titled “Structural basis for protocadherin-1 recognition by New World hantaviruses,” lists Megan M. Slough, Ph.D., of Albert Einstein College of Medicine as first author, with co-authors including Leandro Battini, Ph.D., of Institut Pasteur and Ramandeep Kaur, Ph.D., of LSU Health Shreveport, along with additional contributors from The Henry M. Jackson Foundation, The University of Texas at Austin, University College London, Ichor Biologics LLC, Norgen Biotek, Universidad San Sebastián, the U.S. Army Medical Research Institute of Infectious Diseases, and Fundacion Ciencia & Vida. The work was funded by the National Institutes of Health, through grant U19AI181977 (“PROVIDENT”), and by the European Union (EVH, GAP-101202831). As a structural study, it defines the molecular mechanics of receptor recognition and points to candidate therapeutic strategies, which would still need to be developed and tested before any clinical benefit could be established. 抗体疗法通过中和和 Fc 介导的保护来靶向安第斯山脉病毒 - Bioengineer.org

Antibody therapies target Andes virus through neutralization and Fc-mediated protection - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报This news report, published on October 28, 2026, highlights a review in Biological Research that explores antibody-based therapeutic and prophylactic strategies against the Andes virus. The Andes virus, responsible for hantavirus cardiopulmonary syndrome in South America, has a high case fatality rate of up to 40 percent and lacks a licensed vaccine or antiviral therapy. The review emphasizes that antibody protection against the virus involves both neutralization and Fc-mediated effector functions, which recruit other immune components. It also notes that the virus's unique capacity for person-to-person transmission and its status as a neglected pathogen underscore the urgency for developing effective interventions. The authors summarize current advances in antibody engineering and suggest strategies to enhance therapeutic and prophylactic approaches. The findings are based on evidence from other viral pathogens, which have demonstrated the clinical feasibility of antibody-based interventions.

安第斯病毒主要与南美洲有关,它会引起人类汉坦病毒心肺综合征(汉坦病毒心肺综合征)。
事件
《生物学研究》杂志上发表的一篇评论讨论了针对安第斯病毒的抗体疗法,强调了中和和 Fc 介导的效应功能在 抗病毒药物 免疫和治疗功效中的作用。该事件已根据审查结果得到确认。
规模、影响与风险
The Andes virus has a high case fatality rate, killing up to 40 percent of infected individuals. It is described as a neglected pathogen with significant clinical burden, necessitating the development of therapeutic and prophylactic strategies.
应对、状态与不确定性
该综述概述了基于抗体的疗法的当前进展,并强调了优化抗体工程的策略。由于该病毒独特的传播和高致死率,这也凸显了进一步研究和开发的必要性。

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Antibody therapies target Andes virus through neutralization and Fc-mediated protection by October 28, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 2 mins read 0 Antibody therapies target Andes virus through neutralization and Fc-mediated protection Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Andes virus, the principal cause of hantavirus cardiopulmonary syndrome in South America, kills up to 40 percent of the people it infects, yet no licensed vaccine or specific antiviral therapy exists against it. A review published in the journal Biological Research examines how monoclonal antibodies could fill that gap, arguing that protection against the virus depends not only on the ability of antibodies to neutralize the pathogen directly but also on Fc-mediated effector functions that recruit other arms of the immune system. The authors, led by Jorge T. González, Camila Kossack, José L. Garrido and María I. Barría, summarize current advances in antibody-based therapies targeting the virus and highlight engineering strategies intended to make future interventions more effective. The virus stands out among hantaviruses for a reason that complicates public health responses: it has a unique capacity for person-to-person transmission among hantaviruses. That trait, combined with its high case fatality rate, underscores the need to develop effective therapeutic and prophylactic strategies against the virus, according to the paper. Andes virus is described as a neglected pathogen, and the absence of licensed countermeasures underscores the need for both therapeutic and prophylactic strategies, according to the paper. Monoclonal antibodies have emerged as a promising platform for preventing and treating viral diseases. The review attributes that promise to three characteristics: high specificity for their targets, rapid development timelines compared with traditional vaccine or drug discovery, and the potential for optimization through antibody engineering. Discover more Study Biology Read Med Journals Find Lung Care Seek Counseling Neutralization, the classic mechanism of antibody protection, remains central to the argument. But the review’s central thesis is that neutralization alone does not capture the full picture. The review notes that Fc-mediated effector functions have increasingly been recognized as critical contributors to antiviral immunity and therapeutic efficacy, a shift with consequences for how candidate antibodies against Andes virus should be selected and engineered. The authors also draw on evidence from antibody-based interventions against other viral pathogens, which they say has demonstrated the clinical feasibility of the approach and provides a strong rationale for applying it to Andes virus. The review highlights emerging strategies to optimize antibody engineering for the development of more effective therapeutic and prophylactic interventions against the virus. The review frames its conclusions around a pathogen that has received far less attention than its clinical burden would suggest. 3 people waiting to be medically evacuated from cruise ship at center of suspected hantavirus outbreak - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

B routine priorityPublic-health news

3 people waiting to be medically evacuated from cruise ship at center of suspected hantavirus outbreak - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
A suspected hantavirus outbreak has been reported on the cruise ship MV Hondius, which is currently off the coast of Cape Verde. As of May 6, 2026, seven cases have been identified, including two laboratory-confirmed and five suspected. Three deaths have occurred, involving a married couple from the Netherlands, one of whom was confirmed to have hantavirus. The World Health Organization (WHO) is investigating potential human-to-human transmission, particularly among close contacts, and has assumed the strain may be Andes virus, which has been known to transmit between people. The WHO has stated that the risk to the global population is low. Medical evacuations are being arranged for the affected passengers, with two planes scheduled to transport them to the Netherlands. Health authorities are conducting lab tests and clinical assessments, with a focus on older passengers. Safety measures, including social distancing and masking, are in place on the ship. The outbreak is being managed through an international response, including case isolation and care. The WHO has emphasized the need for full personal protective equipment for those handling patients. The initial cases may have been contracted before boarding, and symptoms can take up to eight weeks to appear. The situation remains under active investigation, with no microbiological tests yet performed on some patients.

OffOn Stream on 3 people waiting to be medically evacuated from cruise ship at center of suspected hantavirus outbreak There are currently two laboratory-confirmed and five suspected cases. ByMary Kekatos and Othon Leyva May 6, 2026, 1:06 AM At least three people are waiting to be medically evacuated from a cruise ship at the center of a suspected hantavirus outbreak. As of Monday, there have been seven cases reported aboard the MV Hondius, of which two are laboratory-confirmed and five are suspected. Among those seven, there have been three deaths, including a married couple from the Netherlands, one of whom has been confirmed to have been infected with hantavirus. The ship is currently off the coast of Cape Verde and medical teams have been assessing two staff members and a close contact of one of the deceased patients. Related American speaks out from cruise ship amid deadly hantavirus outbreak Cape Verde officials told ABC News they are checking on what care the three patients need before transferring them to ambulances and then on flights to the Netherlands. Two planes have been arranged for medical evacuations of the sick passengers from the ship between 4 a.m. and 6 a.m. ET, according to World Health Organization representative for Cape Verde Dr. Ann Lindstrand. The vessel is expected to sail to the Spanish island of Tenerife in Spain, where Spanish health authorities will take over further investigations, including lab testing and clinical assessments, particularly for older passengers, according to Cape Verde officials. Jake Rosmarin, an American passenger on the ship, told ABC News that all the remaining passengers on board remain "in good spirits" and "are hopeful that this situation will be resolved soon." "Safety measures are in place, including social distancing and masking. Passengers also have the option to have meals delivered directly to their cabins," he said. "Access to the outer decks is permitted for fresh air, while gathering in indoor common areas such as the lounge is currently restricted." This aerial picture shows a general view of the cruise ship MV Hondius stationary off the port of Praia, the capital of Cape Verde, on May 5, 2026. AFP via Getty Images It comes as an epidemiologist at the WHO said there may be some person-to-person spread in the outbreak. "We do believe that there may be some human-to-human transmission that's happening among the really close contacts, the husband and wife, people who've shared cabins, [et cetera]," Maria Van Kerkhove, an infectious disease epidemiologist and director of Epidemic and Pandemic Management at the WHO, said during a press conference Tuesday. "So again, our assumption is that has happened, and that's why we are operating and working with the ship to make sure that anyone who is symptomatic, anyone caring for patients, is wearing full personal protective equipment," she added. Van Kerkhove noted that hantaviruses normally don't transmit from person to person because it's a "rodent infection." Patients typically become infected when they come into contact with rodent urine, droppings or saliva, according to the WHO. The initial patients who fell ill may have become infected before they boarded the ship, Van Kerkhove added. Symptoms of hantavirus infections sometimes do not start until eight weeks after contact with the virus, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Related 3 dead in suspected hantavirus outbreak aboard a cruise ship: What to know The WHO is working under the assumption that the strain of hantavirus in the suspected outbreak is the Andes virus, which historically has been shown to potentially transmit between people, although sequencing is still ongoing, according to Van Kerkhove. In a post on its website, the WHO said the onset of illnesses aboard the MV Hondius occurred between April 6 and April 28, with patients experiencing fever, gastrointestinal symptoms, rapid progression to pneumonia, acute respiratory distress syndrome and shock. "The outbreak is being managed through coordinated international response, and includes in-depth investigations, case isolation and care, medical evacuation and laboratory investigations," the WHO wrote in the post. WHO Director-General Dr. Tedros Ghebreysus wrote in a post on X Tuesday that the infection risk to the global population is low and that the agency will continue to monitor the situation and provide updates. Timeline of cases The first suspected hantavirus case occurred in a 70-year-old male passenger from the Netherlands who developed fever, headache and mild diarrhea on April 6, according to the WHO and South African health officials. Popular Reads 2 college students found dead after going missing while hiking, police say 17 hours ago 'Inexplicable' order probed as cause of train crash that killed at least 69: Official 6 hours ago Britt McHenry resigns from State Department after basketball game altercation Oct 23 On April 11, the passenger developed respiratory distress and died on board the ship the same day. His body was removed from the ship to the British territory of St. Helena on April 24. No microbiological tests were performed on the man, according to the WHO. Also on April 24, the male passenger's 69-year-old wife developed gastrointestinal symptoms, health officials said. Her health rapidly declined while on a flight to Johannesburg, South Africa, on April 25 and she died upon arrival to an emergency department the next day, the WHO said. WHO epidemic and pandemic preparedness and prevention chief Maria Van Kerkhove speaking regarding hantavirus cases at the WHO heaquarters in Geneva, May 4, 2026. World Health Organization/AFP via Getty Images On Monday, May 4, laboratory testing confirmed that the wife was infected with hantavirus. Health workers have begun working to identify anyone who may have come into contact with the couple. According to the WHO, the couple had traveled in South America, including Argentina, before they boarded the cruise ship on April 1. During Tuesday's press conference, Van Kerkhove said officials suspect the couple was infected with hantavirus before boarding the ship. "The initial patients, the initial case and his wife, they joined the boat in Argentina. And with the timing of the incubation period of hantavirus, which can be anywhere from one to six weeks, our assumption is that they were infected off the ship, perhaps doing some activities there," she said. A British passenger started developing symptoms on April 24, including shortness of breath and signs of pneumonia, according to the WHO and South African health officials. His condition worsened and he was medically evacuated from Ascension, another British territory, to South Africa on April 27, where he is currently hospitalized in an intensive care unit. Laboratory testing confirmed hantavirus infection over the weekend, the WHO said. Another passenger, an adult female, began experiencing symptoms on April 28, including a general feeling of being unwell, according to the WHO. She later presented with pneumonia and died on May 2. Oceanwide Expeditions, which operates the cruise ship, previously revealed that the patient was a German national. In addition, there are three suspected cases currently onboard the MV Hondius reporting high fever and/or gastrointestinal symptoms, the WHO said. The ship is currently off the coast of Cape Verde, where medical teams in the area are evaluating the patients and collecting additional specimens for testing, according to the WHO. Van Kerkhove said the highest priority is to medically evacuate the symptomatic individuals onboard the ship so they receive the care they need. "The plan now is for the ship to continue on to the Canary Islands. We're working with Spanish authorities who will welcome the ship, have said that they will welcome the ship, to do a full investigation, a full epidemiological investigation, full disinfection of the ship, and of course to assess the risk of the passengers that are actually on board," she said. "The medical evacuation of two individuals currently requiring urgent medical care, and the individual associated with the guest who passed away on 2 May, will occur using two specialized aircraft that are en route to Cape Verde," Oceanwide Expeditions said in a statement Tuesday. "From here, the patients are to be medically evacuated to the Netherlands," the statement continued, noting that there currently was no "exact timeline" for the evacuation. The company added that once the guests have been safely transferred and are in transit to the Netherlands, the ship will proceed to the Canary Islands. "[T]he hospital has already prepared an area with isolation capacity, should it be necessary," Angela Gomes, Cape Verde's national director of health, said in a statement Tuesday. "A dedicated team has been formed, with doctors, infectious disease specialists, nurses and laboratory technicians, to support patients on board and, if necessary, also on land." The Spanish Ministry of Health said Spain will host the MV Hondius vessel in the Canary Islands in compliance with "international law and the humanitarian spirit." Once there, crew and passengers will be examined, treated and transferred to their respective countries, the ministry said. Following this same logic, and as part of the operation, the Government has also accepted a formal request from the Government of the Netherlands to take in the doctor from the MV Hondius, who is in serious condition and will be transported to the Canary Islands on a hospital plane today. ABC News' Emily Cohen and Dada Jovanovic contributed to this report. WHO says suspected hantavirus cluster 'is not the start of a COVID pandemic' - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报世界卫生组织 (WHO) 解决了一艘游轮上疑似汉坦病毒聚集性病例,澄清这并不是新冠疫情大流行的开始。暴发 事件发生在 2026 年 4 月 11 日至 5 月 2 日期间,涉及 8 例病例,其中 5 例确诊,3 例疑似,据报告 3 人死亡。MV Hondius 号游轮曾前往荷兰、新加坡和美国等 12 个国家。世卫组织强调,汉坦病毒通过密切、亲密接触而不是人与人之间传播,应对措施包括防止进一步传播的预防措施。在荷兰,两名患者正在住院治疗,而在新加坡,两名居民正在接受监测。疾病预防控制中心预计将提供技术援助,并正在对下机的乘客进行接触者追踪。一些人的检测结果仍在等待中,这名德国妇女的确切死因仍不清楚。世界卫生组织表示,对公众的风险较低,截至 2026 年 5 月 7 日,在美国接受监测的乘客中尚未报告病例。

时间
疑似汉坦病毒集群于2026年5月7日报告,事件日期为2026年4月11日至5月2日,在此期间发现了病例并发生了死亡。发布日期为2026年10月24日。
地点与对象
疑似汉坦病毒集群发生在一艘游轮上,该游轮前往12个国家,包括加拿大、瑞士、德国、丹麦、英国、圣基茨和尼维斯、荷兰、新西兰、新加坡、瑞典、土耳其和美国。受影响的人群包括来自这些国家的乘客,特别提到了亚利桑那州、佐治亚州和美国其他地区的监控。
事件
世界卫生组织 (WHO) 确认一艘游轮上出现疑似汉坦病毒聚集性病例,但并未将其归类为新冠疫情大流行的开始。该聚集性病例涉及 8 例,其中 5 例为实验室确诊病例,3 例为疑似病例。三人死亡,其中包括一对荷兰夫妇和一名德国妇女。

应对、状态与不确定性
世界卫生组织正在与各国当局协调,预计疾病预防控制中心将提供技术援助。正在对下机乘客进行接触者追踪和风险评估。一些人的检测结果正在等待中,这名德国妇女的确切死因尚不清楚。

WHO says suspected hantavirus cluster 'is not the start of a COVID pandemic' - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) addressed a suspected hantavirus cluster on a cruise ship, clarifying that it is not the start of a new COVID pandemic. The outbreak, which occurred between April 11 and May 2, 2026, involved eight cases, five confirmed and three suspected, with three deaths reported. The cruise ship, MV Hondius, traveled to 12 countries, including the Netherlands, Singapore, and the United States. The WHO emphasized that hantavirus spreads through close, intimate contact rather than person-to-person transmission, and the response includes precautionary measures to prevent further spread. In the Netherlands, two patients are hospitalized, while in Singapore, two residents are being monitored. The CDC is expected to provide technical assistance, and contact tracing is ongoing for passengers who disembarked. Test results for some individuals are pending, and the exact cause of death for the German woman remains unknown. The WHO stated that the risk to the general public is low, and no cases have been reported among the monitored passengers in the U.S. as of May 7, 2026.

OffOn Stream on WHO says suspected hantavirus cluster 'is not the start of a COVID pandemic' There are 12 suspected cases so far. ByMary Kekatos and Zoe Magee May 7, 2026, 4:39 PM Share An epidemiologist from the World Health Organization (WHO) said Thursday that the suspected hantavirus cluster aboard a cruise ship is not the beginning of another COVID-19 pandemic. Eight cases are currently being reported by the WHO, including five laboratory-confirmed cases and three suspected cases. Of those eight cases, three have died. Dr. Maria Van Kerkhove, an infectious disease epidemiologist and acting director of epidemic and pandemic management at the WHO, was asked during a press conference what the difference was between this cluster and the early days of the COVID pandemic. Related Officials in Arizona, Georgia monitoring some hantavirus cruise ship passengers who have returned to US "I want to be unequivocal here. This is not SARS-CoV-2. This is not the start of a COVID pandemic. This is an outbreak that we see on a ship," Van Kerkhove said. Van Kerkhove explained that hantavirus doesn't spread in the same way that coronaviruses do, but rather through "close, intimate contact." Most hantaviruses don't transmit from person to person. "The actions that are being taken on board [the ship] are precautionary to prevent any onward spread," she added. Health personnel assisting patients onto a boat from the cruise ship MV Hondius, while stationary off the port of Praia, the capital of Cape Verde, May 6, 2026. AFP via Getty Images There appears to be two confirmed cases and four suspected cases that have not been added to the WHO's official count yet. The Ministry of Health, Welfare and Sport confirmed that two patients infected with the virus are currently hospitalized in the Netherlands, one at Radboud University Medical Center and the other at Leiden University Medical Center. Additionally, two Singapore residents who were on board the ship are currently being monitored. Singapore's Communicable Diseases Agency said it was notified of the individuals on May 4 and 5. "They have been isolated at the National Centre for Infectious Diseases, where they are being tested for hantavirus. The risk to the general public in Singapore is currently low," the agency said. The agency added that test results are pending, with one resident having a runny nose. The other is asymptomatic. The French Health Ministry also issued a statement on Thursday that a French national has been tested after they displayed mild symptoms. This person had been in contact with one of the confirmed cases. Related 3 people waiting to be medically evacuated from cruise ship at center of suspected hantavirus outbreak Three deaths have been recorded so far, including a married Dutch couple. The 70-year-old male patient died on April 11, and his body was taken off the ship on the island of St. Helena on April 24. His 69-year-old wife disembarked on the same day, and her health rapidly deteriorated. She died at a South Africa hospital on April 26. A third passenger, a German woman, presented with pneumonia symptoms starting on April 28, according to the WHO. The woman died on May 2 from causes not yet known, according to Oceanwide Expeditions, which operates the cruise ship. The WHO said 29 people disembarked on St. Helena on the same day that the body of the Dutch male patient and his wife disembarked. They traveled to 12 countries: Canada, Switzerland, Germany, Denmark, Great Britain, St. Kitts and Nevis, Netherlands, New Zealand, Singapore, Sweden, Turkey and the United States. Popular Reads 2 college students found dead after going missing while hiking, police say 17 hours ago 'Inexplicable' order probed as cause of train crash that killed at least 69: Official 6 hours ago Britt McHenry resigns from State Department after basketball game altercation Oct 23 The disembarked guests have all been contacted by Oceanwide Expeditions. In the U.S., local authorities in five states -- Arizona, California, Georgia, Texas and Virginia -- are monitoring the disembarked passengers and are conducting contact tracing, None have shown signs of illness at this time. Anais Legend, technical lead for viral hemorrhagic fevers at WHO, said during the press conference on Thursday that "step-by-step guidance is being developed" for the disembarked passengers and that the WHO is coordinating with national authorities. Anyone with any signs of symptoms will be isolated while other passengers have their risk exposure evaluated. The cruise ship MV Hondius, carrying passengers suspected of having cases of hantavirus on board, leaves Praia, Cape Verde, May 6, 2026. Reuters Public health experts said they expected a more robust response from the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) and the National Institutes of Health. "CDC would typically be asked by WHO to, or by a country to, help in technical assistance," Dr. Carlos del Rio, an H. Cliff Sauls distinguished professor of medicine at the Emory University School of Medicine, told reporters on Thursday. Typically, CDC teams would be deployed to an area, he said, and the teams would perform contact tracing and interviews and conduct an outbreak investigation. "I would envision by now, many, many days ago, we would have seen ... a team from CDC deployed to the area," he added. Related 3 dead in suspected hantavirus outbreak aboard a cruise ship: What to know Dr. Jeanne Marrazzo, CEO of the IDSA, added that she would have expected a CDC press briefing, an alert from the agency's Health Alert Network or information from the NIH on potential treatments in the pipeline that could receive emergency use authorization to help treat hantavirus patients. Marrazzo said she is not aware that conversations about potential therapies at NIH aren't happening but it "does not give me a lot of assurance or reassurance that we're not hearing about any of it." The CDC posted a statement on X on Wednesday on behalf of NIH Director and acting CDC director Dr. Jay Bhattacharya that read, "Our hearts go out to the passengers aboard the cruise ship M/V Hondius and their families during this difficult time. Our CDC team began coordinating with domestic and international partners as soon as we were notified of a hantavirus situation." The statement said there is "clear, written health guidance to the American passengers through the State Department" and that the CDC "will continue to update as more information becomes available and remains committed to protecting the health and safety of the American public." The agency also published a statement on its website stating that the Trump administration "is closely monitoring the situation" and that the Department of State "is leading a coordinated, whole-of-government response including direct contact with passengers, diplomatic coordination, and engagement with domestic and international health authorities." The CDC also said the risk to the American public is "extremely low." The WHO said during Thursday's press briefing that the U.S. is coordinating with the global health agency in a technical capacity. Because the cluster is limited and confined to a cruise ship, the "idea of sending messages across the world and panicking everyone is not required," said Dr. Abdirahman Mahmoud, director of the WHO's health emergency alert and response operations. He added that the WHO is "informally" aware that contact tracing has been done of the U.S. passengers who disembarked last month and are back home. The affected population includes passengers from these countries, with specific mention of monitoring in Arizona, Georgia, and other U.S.
Event
The World Health Organization (WHO) confirmed a suspected hantavirus cluster on a cruise ship, which was not classified as the start of a new COVID pandemic. The cluster involved eight cases, five of which were laboratory-confirmed, and three suspected cases. Three individuals died, including a married Dutch couple and a German woman.
Scale, impact and risk
There are eight reported cases, with three deaths. The WHO stated that the risk to the general public is low, and the cruise ship passengers are being monitored. The virus spreads through close, intimate contact rather than person-to-person transmission, and the actions taken are precautionary to prevent onward spread.
Response, status and uncertainty
The WHO is coordinating with national authorities, and the CDC is expected to provide technical assistance. Contact tracing and risk evaluation are ongoing for the disembarked passengers. Test results for some individuals are pending, and the exact cause of death for the German woman is not yet known.

对 LLM汉坦病毒暴发 回答的专家分析发现 11.1% 存在潜在误导性 - Bioengineer.org

Expert analysis of LLM hantavirus outbreak responses finds 11.1% potentially misleading - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报专家对 2026 年安第斯山脉病毒 暴发 期间三种大型语言模型 (LLM)(ChatGPT、Gemini 和 Claude)生成的 900 个回复进行了分析,发现 11.1% 的回复部分正确或可能具有误导性。该研究于 2026 年 10 月 25 日发表,评估了这些模型如何传达汉坦病毒相关风险信息,重点关注准确性、完整性和安全性。虽然 63.1% 的回答完全正确且充分,25.8% 的回答大部分正确但不完整,但研究人员强调,在公共卫生背景下使用此类信息之前需要经过专家审查。该分析没有评估患者的结果或临床效果,而是将模型输出与官方健康指南进行比较。结果表明,当法学硕士的产出以官方信息为基础并由专家监测时,它们可以支持风险沟通,但它们不能取代临床判断或作为复杂健康指导的独立来源。该研究强调了确保大多数正确答案不会掩盖单个模型输出中误导性内容的可能性的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究是在2026年安第斯山脉病毒暴发期间进行的,发表日期为2026年10月25日。
地点与对象
该分析重点关注 2026 年安第斯山脉病毒 暴发 期间大型语言模型 (LLM) 生成的响应,该响应与汉坦病毒相关的风险沟通相关。
事件
对 900 份 LLM 生成的回复进行专家评级分析后发现,关于 2026 年安第斯病毒 暴发 期间的汉坦病毒风险沟通,11.1% 部分正确或可能具有误导性。该研究评估了 ChatGPT、Gemini 和 Claude 的输出,而不是患者的结果或临床效果。
规模、影响与风险
在 900 份回复中,568 份完全正确且充分,232 份大部分正确但不完整,100 份部分正确或可能具有误导性。该研究强调,在公开使用之前需要对模型生成的 暴发 信息进行审查,因为近九分之一的答案属于误导性类别。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是使用预先定义的标准和专家评估进行的,并以描述性方式报告结果。研究结果表明,虽然大多数回答都是准确的,但误导信息的可能性仍然存在,并且模型的输出需要专家监督才能进行可靠的风险沟通。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Expert analysis of LLM hantavirus outbreak responses finds 11.1% potentially misleading - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
An expert analysis of 900 responses generated by three large language models (LLMs)—ChatGPT, Gemini, and Claude—during the 2026 Andes virus outbreak found that 11.1% of the responses were partially correct or potentially misleading. The study, published on October 25, 2026, evaluated how these models communicated hantavirus-related risk information, focusing on accuracy, completeness, and safety. While 63.1% of responses were completely correct and sufficient, and 25.8% were mostly correct but incomplete, the researchers emphasized the need for expert scrutiny before using such information in public health contexts. The analysis did not assess patient outcomes or clinical effects but instead compared model outputs against official health guidelines. The results suggest that LLMs can support risk communication when their outputs are grounded in official information and monitored by experts, but they cannot replace clinical judgment or serve as standalone sources for complex health guidance. The study highlights the importance of ensuring that the majority of correct responses do not mask the possibility of misleading content in individual model outputs.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 30, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Expert analysis of LLM hantavirus outbreak responses finds 11.1% potentially misleading by October 25, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Expert analysis of LLM hantavirus outbreak responses finds 11.1% potentially misleading Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram An expert-rated analysis of 900 responses generated by three publicly accessible large language models found that 63.1% were completely correct and sufficient, while 11.1% were partially correct or potentially misleading about hantavirus risk communication during the 2026 Andes virus outbreak. The comparative, cross-sectional study assessed outputs from ChatGPT, Gemini and Claude rather than patient outcomes, disease transmission or the models’ effects in a clinical setting. Across the evaluated dataset, no response was rated completely incorrect or overtly harmful, but the findings indicate that model-generated outbreak information still requires scrutiny before public use. The researchers submitted 150 predefined hantavirus-related questions to the non-Pro, publicly accessible versions of the three models. The questions covered three areas: information based on official health-authority or guideline material, expert and clinical-practice issues, and frequently asked public questions. Each question was submitted twice, using either a standard zero-shot prompt or a prompt directed toward public-health risk communication. The two prompt formats generated 900 responses altogether, providing a dataset for comparing model outputs and assessing whether specialized risk-communication framing changed the expert-rated distribution of answers. Three independent experts evaluated the responses with a predefined rubric addressing accuracy, completeness, consistency, safety and actionability. Ratings were summarized descriptively, while inter-rater reliability was assessed with percentage agreement and Fleiss’ kappa. Of the 900 responses, 568 were classified as completely correct and sufficient. A further 232, or 25.8%, were rated mostly correct but incomplete, and 100, or 11.1%, were considered partially correct or potentially misleading. Thus, 88.9% received one of the two higher ratings, even though nearly one in nine answers fell into the category that allowed potentially misleading information. Discover more Learn AI Tools Learn a Language Learn to Code Read Med Journals Find Lung Care ChatGPT had the numerically highest proportion of completely correct responses, with 198 of 300 answers, or 66.0%, and the lowest proportion in the potentially misleading category, with 28 of 300, or 9.3%. Gemini and Claude had completely correct rates of 62.7% and 60.7%, respectively. The investigators interpreted these model-level differences descriptively and did not present them as evidence that one system was superior. The aggregate distribution of categorical ratings also did not change with the public-health risk communication prompt compared with the standard zero-shot prompt, indicating no measured change in the overall rating pattern under the two tested formats. The study supports a qualified role for publicly accessible language models in infectious-disease and outbreak-related communication, but its results do not establish that the systems can serve as stand-alone sources of clinically complex or rapidly changing guidance. The analysis was confined to 150 predefined questions, three public model systems, two prompt formats and an expert scoring framework, and its results were reported descriptively rather than through statistical claims of model superiority. The researchers concluded that these tools may support risk communication when their outputs are grounded in official information and subjected to expert oversight. During a hantavirus outbreak, that distinction remains important because a majority of correct or mostly correct answers does not eliminate the possibility of incomplete or potentially misleading content in individual responses. View five news elements
Time
The study was conducted during the 2026 Andes virus outbreak, with the publication date being October 25, 2026.
Location and population
The analysis focused on responses generated by large language models (LLMs) during the 2026 Andes virus outbreak, which is associated with hantavirus-related risk communication.
Event
An expert-rated analysis of 900 LLM-generated responses found that 11.1% were partially correct or potentially misleading regarding hantavirus risk communication during the 2026 Andes virus outbreak. The study assessed outputs from ChatGPT, Gemini, and Claude rather than patient outcomes or clinical effects.
Scale, impact and risk
Of the 900 responses, 568 were completely correct and sufficient, 232 were mostly correct but incomplete, and 100 were partially correct or potentially misleading. The study emphasized the need for scrutiny of model-generated outbreak information before public use, as nearly one in nine answers fell into the misleading category.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted using a predefined rubric and expert evaluation, with results reported descriptively. The findings indicate that while the majority of responses were accurate, the potential for misleading information remains, and the models’ outputs require expert oversight for reliable risk communication.

随着美国撤离美国人,汉坦病毒游轮上的乘客在加那利群岛下船

Passengers on hantavirus cruise disembark in Canary Islands as US evacuates Americans - The Post Millennial
新闻简报停靠在加那利群岛特内里费岛附近的MV Hondius游轮上的一架汉坦病毒 暴发导致近150名乘客和船员疏散。与三例死亡有关的 暴发 被确定为安第斯山脉病毒,这是唯一已知能够人传人的 传播 病毒。西班牙官员于 2026 年 5 月 10 日开始撤离乘客,第一架撤侨航班载有 14 名西班牙乘客前往马德里接受隔离。其他航班将乘客运送到荷兰、土耳其、英国、爱尔兰和美国。世界卫生组织确认了八例疑似病例中的六例,船上没有剩余疑似病例。撤离人员报告称,整个过程很顺利,但 暴发 的全部范围及其收容措施仍在调查中。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件发生于 2026 年 5 月 10 日,该船于 2026 年 10 月 29 日抵达特内里费岛附近,疏散工作持续到 2026 年 10 月 29 日这一周。
地点与对象
MV Hondius 游轮位于加那利群岛,特别是特内里费岛附近。受影响的人群包括近 150 名乘客和机组人员,其中至少有 10 名加拿大人和其他国际旅客。
事件
MV Hondius 游轮上的汉坦病毒暴发 导致乘客和船员从加那利群岛撤离。暴发 与三例死亡有关,确定的毒株是安第斯病毒,该病毒具有人传人的 传播 能力。
规模、影响与风险
据报道,至少三人死亡,其中八例疑似病例中有六例确诊。暴发 促使国际社会采取卫生应对措施,包括遣返航班和军队医院的隔离措施。该风险被指出与安第斯病毒有关,该病毒是唯一已知的具有人传人 传播 能力的 汉坦病毒。
应对、状态与不确定性
西班牙当局启动了大规模疏散行动,乘客被较小的船只和军用巴士转移到南特内里费机场。世界卫生组织确认了八例疑似病例中的六例,船上没有报告其余疑似病例。一些撤离人员将情况描述为“一切进展顺利”,但 暴发 的确切状态及其收容措施仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Passengers on hantavirus cruise disembark in Canary Islands as US evacuates Americans - The Post Millennial

News Brief
A hantavirus outbreak on the cruise ship MV Hondius, which was docked near Tenerife in the Canary Islands, led to the evacuation of nearly 150 passengers and crew. The outbreak, linked to three deaths, was identified as the Andes virus, the only known hantavirus capable of human-to-human transmission. Spanish officials began removing passengers on May 10, 2026, with the first evacuation flight carrying 14 Spanish passengers to Madrid for quarantine. Additional flights transported passengers to the Netherlands, Turkey, the UK, Ireland, and the US. The World Health Organization confirmed six cases out of eight suspected ones, with no remaining suspected cases aboard. Evacuees reported the process was smooth, but the full extent of the outbreak and its containment remains under investigation.

By Roberto Wakerell-Cruz·May 10, 2026·2 min read Passengers from the cruise ship MV Hondius began returning home Sunday after Spanish authorities launched a large-scale evacuation from the Canary Islands following a hantavirus outbreak linked to three deaths. by Taboolaby Taboola Sponsored LinksSponsored Links Promoted LinksPromoted Links You May Like BREAKING: Dem Kissimmee mayor arrested on grand theft, fraud charges: AG UthmeierKissimmee Mayor Jackie Espinosa was arrested on Wednesday morning. Undo The ship arrived near Tenerife after multiple passengers became infected with the rare virus, prompting an international health response. Three people have died so far, including a Dutch husband and wife and a German woman. Spanish officials began removing passengers from the Dutch-flagged vessel Sunday morning. Travelers wearing blue protective suits were transferred by smaller boats to the port of Granadilla before being taken by Spanish military buses to Tenerife South airport for repatriation flights. The first flight carried 14 Spanish passengers to Madrid, where they will quarantine at a military hospital. “Everything is going well,” French evacuee Roland Seitre told AFP before departure, adding that “everyone was great” during the disembarkation. A separate flight transported 27 passengers to the Netherlands, including citizens from Belgium, Greece, Germany, Guatemala, and Argentina, according to Spanish civil protection chief Virginia Barcones. Additional flights for Turkish, British, Irish, and American passengers were also scheduled. Spanish Health Minister Monica Garcia said most of the ship’s nearly 150 passengers and crew were expected to be evacuated before a final repatriation flight to Australia on Monday. Health authorities have identified at least 10 affected Canadians. Four remained onboard before the vessel reached the Canary Islands, while six others were either no longer aboard or had close contact with infected individuals. Canadian passengers departing Sunday were seen wearing protective equipment while boarding a flight bound for Saguenay-Bagotville Airport in Quebec. The outbreak has drawn attention because the strain is the Andes virus, the only known hantavirus capable of human-to-human transmission. The World Health Organization said Friday it had confirmed six cases out of eight suspected ones and reported no remaining suspected cases aboard the ship. Location and population
The cruise ship MV Hondius was in the Canary Islands, specifically near Tenerife. The affected population included nearly 150 passengers and crew, with at least 10 Canadians and other international travelers.
Event
A hantavirus outbreak on the cruise ship MV Hondius led to the evacuation of passengers and crew from the Canary Islands. The outbreak was linked to three deaths, and the strain identified was the Andes virus, which is capable of human-to-human transmission.
Scale, impact and risk
At least three deaths were reported, with six confirmed cases out of eight suspected ones. 西班牙将接受载有汉坦病毒 暴发的邮轮 - Turkmenportal.com

Spain to accept cruise ship with hantavirus outbreak on board - Turkmenportal.com
新闻简报根据世界卫生组织的要求并与欧盟协调,西班牙已同意接受一艘载有汉坦病毒暴发 的游轮。该船目前停泊在佛得角海岸,船上有147人。暴发 已导致 7 例确诊感染,3 例死亡,1 例患者情况危急。欧洲疾病预防和控制中心(ECDC)将在该船抵达西班牙港口之前进行情况评估,其余乘客将继续前往加那利群岛。抵达后,所有乘客都将接受体检、接受必要的护理,然后被遣返。这一决定反映了西班牙对管理与该病毒相关的公共卫生风险的承诺,该病毒是通过直接接触或身体分泌物从啮齿动物传播给人类的。
查看新闻五要素
时间
西班牙同意接收一艘载有汉坦病毒的游轮,该船目前停泊在佛得角海岸,预计将在三到四天内抵达加那利群岛。世界卫生组织确认了七例感染病例,三例死亡,以及一名重症患者,其中暴发报告于30.10.2026。
地点与对象
该游轮目前停泊在佛得角海岸,船上有147人。该船预计将抵达加那利群岛,但具体停靠港尚未确定。
事件
暴发 已导致 7 例确诊感染、3 例死亡和 1 例重症患者。
规模、影响与风险
暴发已影响游轮上的147人,其中7例确诊为汉坦病毒感染病例,3人死亡,1人情况危急。欧洲疾病预防控制中心将在该船抵达西班牙港口前进行情况评估,其余乘客将继续前往加那利群岛。
应对、状态与不确定性
西班牙已同意接收该游轮,所有乘客抵达后将接受体检。ECDC 将在该船抵达西班牙港口之前进行情况评估。具体停靠港尚未确定,应对措施以世卫组织协调为准。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Spain to accept cruise ship with hantavirus outbreak on board - Turkmenportal.com

News Brief
Spain has agreed to accept a cruise ship with a hantavirus outbreak on board, as requested by the World Health Organization and coordinated with the European Union. The ship is currently moored off the coast of Cape Verde, with 147 people on board. The outbreak has resulted in seven confirmed infections, three deaths, and one patient in critical condition. The European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) will conduct a situation assessment before the ship arrives in a Spanish port, and the remaining passengers will continue on to the Canary Islands. Upon arrival, all passengers will undergo a medical examination, receive necessary care, and be repatriated. The decision reflects Spain's commitment to managing public health risks associated with the virus, which is transmitted from rodents to humans through direct contact or their bodily secretions.

The decision was made at the request of the World Health Organization, in coordination with the European Union, MIR24 reports. The ship is currently moored off the coast of Cape Verde. Its arrival in the Canary Islands is expected in three to four days; a specific port of call has not yet been determined. There are 147 people on board. According to the WHO, seven of them have been confirmed infected: three have died, and one patient is in critical condition. Before the ship arrives in a Spanish port, the European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control (ECDC) will conduct a situation assessment. Based on this assessment, it will determine who requires emergency evacuation from Cape Verde; the remaining passengers will continue on to the Canary Islands. Upon arrival, all on board will undergo a medical examination, receive the necessary care, and be repatriated. Spain also granted a separate request from the Netherlands: a doctor from the plane, whose condition is considered serious, will be transported to the Canary Islands on a special medical flight. 我可以从我的宠物身上得到汉坦病毒吗?以下是宠物主人应该了解的内容 - ABC 新闻 - 突发新闻、最新新闻和视频

Can I get hantavirus from my pet? Here's what pet owners should know - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos
新闻简报ABC 新闻的这篇文章解决了宠物主人对其宠物感染汉坦病毒的可能性的担忧。它解释说,虽然猫和狗可能感染 汉坦病毒,但它们通常不会表现出症状,并且不太可能将病毒传播给人类。美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 指出,汉坦病毒 在美国很少见,大多数病例报告于加利福尼亚州、科罗拉多州、新墨西哥州和亚利桑那州等特定州。该文章强调,主要风险来自于接触受污染的颗粒(通常来自野生啮齿动物),并建议减少家庭附近的啮齿动物活动。它还提到了安第斯病毒,这是一种主要在南美洲发现的罕见病毒,可以在人与人之间传播,但目前尚不清楚是否可以通过宠物传播。疾病预防控制中心建议清洁笼子,使其远离准备食物的地方,在处理啮齿类动物时使用口罩和手套,如果怀疑有接触,请立即就医。文章强调,目前尚无针对宠物的汉坦病毒测试,预防重点是尽量减少啮齿类动物的接触。
查看新闻五要素
时间

美国,宠物主人。
事件
建议宠物主人注意宠物感染汉坦病毒传播 的风险,专家表示猫和狗通常不会表现出症状,也不太可能感染人类。文章还提到安第斯病毒是一种罕见的例外,可以在人与人之间传播,但目前尚不清楚宠物是否会将其传播给人类。
规模、影响与风险
汉坦病毒 在美国仍然罕见且分散,大多数病例报告于加利福尼亚州、科罗拉多州、新墨西哥州和亚利桑那州。感染的风险取决于啮齿动物的活动和接触受污染颗粒的情况,例如在清洁或户外活动期间。疾病预防控制中心指出,早期症状类似于流感,感染可能会变得严重,影响肺部。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章讨论了预防措施,包括密封孔洞、用消毒剂清洁啮齿动物粪便、密封宠物食品以及一旦接触就立即就医。它还提到没有对宠物进行汉坦病毒测试,并且 CDC 建议谨慎接触啮齿类动物。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Can I get hantavirus from my pet? Here's what pet owners should know - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
This article from ABC News addresses concerns among pet owners about the possibility of contracting hantavirus from their pets. It explains that while cats and dogs can be infected with hantavirus, they typically do not show symptoms and are unlikely to transmit the virus to humans. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) notes that hantavirus is rare in the U.S., with most cases reported in specific states like California, Colorado, New Mexico, and Arizona. The article highlights that the primary risk comes from exposure to contaminated particles, often from wild rodents, and advises reducing rodent activity near homes. It also mentions the Andes virus, a rare strain found mainly in South America, which can spread from person to person but is not known to be transmitted by pets. The CDC recommends cleaning cages away from food preparation, using masks and gloves when handling rodents, and seeking immediate medical attention if exposure is suspected. The article emphasizes that there is no test for hantavirus in pets, and prevention focuses on minimizing rodent contact.

OffOn Stream on News Can I get hantavirus from my pet? Here's what pet owners should know Global race to contain hantavirus outbreak Simpleimages/STOCK PHOTO/Getty Images ByDr. Geraldine Nabeta May 14, 2026, 10:25 PM The recent headlines about illnesses and deaths tied to possible hantavirus exposure have left some pet owners wondering whether the virus can be spread through the furry creature sleeping on their couch or curled up on their lap. While cats and dogs can be infected with the hantavirus, they generally don’t have symptoms and experts say they are highly unlikely to infect their owners. Related Passenger from hantavirus-stricken cruise ship speaks out from quarantine "They can be exposed, but they don't get sick the way that we know people to get sick," Dr. Kate Elden, the chief medical officer of the veterinary telehealth company, Dutch, told ABC News. "There's no actual test that a veterinarian or your local vet is going to have." And even when a dog or cat does have a hantavirus infection, they don’t transfer the virus to humans, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. However, if they bring an infected wild rodent into the home that could theoretically increase your exposure, Elden cautioned. Stock photo of a dog and cat being fed. Simpleimages/STOCK PHOTO/Getty Images "Don't hesitate to call your vet if your pet brought a rodent home and starts to [look] unwell," she said. Elden advised disposing of any rodent your pet has brought home while wearing a mask and gloves, and then thoroughly washing hands with soap and water after it has safely been dispatched. Even owners of pet rats and mice don’t have much to worry about, Elden said. "The domestic hamster, guinea pig, gerbil, these are captive bred animals, we have no documentation that they play a role in hantavirus cases or are part of spreading the disease," Elden said. To be safe, clean cages away from food preparation and spray down bedding to minimize dust particles in the air if necessary, she advised. Popular Reads 2 college students found dead after going missing while hiking, police say 17 hours ago 'Inexplicable' order probed as cause of train crash that killed at least 69: Official 6 hours ago Britt McHenry resigns from State Department after basketball game altercation Oct 23 Deer mice are the most likely natural hosts for hantavirus, the CDC notes. Related American who survived hantavirus in 1993 outbreak describes her symptoms and recovery The virus lives in their bodies without making them sick, and they spread it through their urine, droppings and saliva, according to the American Veterinary Medical Association. But your risk depends a lot on where you live. Hantavirus remain rare and scattered in the U.S., according to the CDC, with most cases reported in California, Colorado, New Mexico and Arizona. People typically become infected after inhaling contaminated particles, usually while cleaning sheds, cabins, garages or rodent-infested areas or while doing outdoor activities like hunting and camping. One rare exception is the Andes virus, a strain found mainly in South America that can spread from person to person through close contact and has been linked to the recent cruise ship cases. Pets are not known to spread Andes virus to humans. The CDC lists early symptoms of hantavirus similar to those resembling the flu, including fever, muscle aches, fatigue, headache, nausea and shortness of breath. The infection can quickly take a turn, becoming more severe and affecting the lungs. Related Former White House COVID-19 response coordinator answers hantavirus questions The best way to avoid infection is by reducing rodent activity near your home, Elden said. Seal any holes and clean rodent droppings with a disinfectant instead of sweeping or vacuuming. Keep pet food sealed. And if you think you've been exposed through any source, the CDC recommends seeking immediate medical attention. Geraldine Nabeta MD MSc is an incoming chief resident in internal medicine at the University of Connecticut and a member of the ABC News Medical Unit. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Officials in Arizona, Georgia monitoring some hantavirus cruise ship passengers who have returned to US - ABC News - Breaking News, Latest News and Videos

News Brief
Health authorities in the U.S. are monitoring some passengers who returned from a cruise ship suspected of a hantavirus outbreak. The cruise ship, MV Hondius, was off the coast of Cape Verde and has been medically evacuated. As of May 7, 2026, five confirmed hantavirus cases have been reported, with three deaths recorded. Over 100 passengers remain on the ship, and the WHO is working with the ship's operators to monitor their health. The CDC stated that the risk to the American public is extremely low, but some person-to-person spread may occur. Officials in Arizona and Georgia are monitoring two residents who returned home after disembarking. None of the monitored individuals have shown signs of illness. The Department of State is coordinating a whole-of-government response, including contact tracing and medical follow-up. The WHO confirmed the evacuations and emphasized that the overall public health risk remains low. The CDC urged passengers to follow health guidelines, and some passengers have been advised to self-isolate.
